# Backend gaps

What a screen needs that the backend doesn't serve. A screen is never reshaped
to fit the current API; the gap is written here, with the artboard and field,
and becomes (or joins) a backend task.

The analysis already turned 136 gaps into tasks (see `tasks/INDEX.md` and
`docs/analysis/ANALYSIS-EVIDENCE.md`, "Backend build list"). Add new ones
below as screens are built.

| # | artboard | field or behaviour needed | found by | task |
|---|----------|---------------------------|----------|------|
| G-1 | all | The backend contract repeats inline response shapes per route (`FlutterwaveConfig` ten times, `Items` four times) instead of named schemas, so clients cannot share one type. Publish named schemas. MONEY-04 did this for the wallet routes (every `/money/*` body is named, checked by `src/money/tests/money-contract.spec.ts`). Still open for the served routes: the Flutterwave checkout config inline 9 times, Prisma rows inlined (a legal request 14 times), `Paginated<T>` inlined 12 times, and 11 nullable named fields written `{ $ref, nullable }`, which generated clients read as never null (list in wawu-backend `docs/contract/WALLET.md`, section 5). Retypes live routes' contract entries, so it needs a V3 run. **LEGAL-01:** the chat-first legal routes (`/legal/assistant/*`, `GET /legal/ops/intakes/{id}/assistant`) are new and publish only named schemas: the matter options the assistant offers are `LegalAssistantTopic` (`GET /legal/assistant/topics`), the conversation `LegalAssistantThread`, its messages, taps, brief and consultant each a named schema, and a nullable named field is written `allOf` so it reads as nullable. The older legal routes are unchanged. | setup, 1 Oct 2026 | MONEY-04 (wallet routes); served routes: none yet (owner to assign) |
| G-2 | all (sign-up) | wawu-id `POST /auth/register` returns an `accessToken` (and refresh token) before the email is confirmed, so the Hub accepts calls from an account whose email was never proven; only `/auth/login` checks `emailVerified`. Decide whether register should issue tokens before confirmation. Mobile does not use it. `POST /auth/signup` issues no token, and since AUTH-03 round 2 no wawu-id route (sign-in, email confirmation, phone OTP, password reset, refresh) gives a session to an account whose sign-up phone code is still pending. `register` itself is unchanged for the web, still issues tokens before any confirmation, and the Hub still accepts them: that part of this gap stays open for the web. | OPS-03 verifier, 2 Oct 2026 | AUTH-03 (mobile sign-up done, `register` unchanged for the web, R-36); web register: none yet (owner to assign) |

| G-6 | Settings (sign out) | wawu-id has no sign-out route. | G-10 | W39 (earnings), Z6 to Z8 (delete account), refunds | A frozen Fintava wallet can neither send nor receive: a wallet-to-wallet send into it answers `400 "Kindly confirm both customer accounts are active"` (OPS-02, `docs/fintava/sandbox/15-freeze-unfreeze.md`). So earnings (R-11) and refunds (R-19) sent to a frozen wallet are refused, not held, and the 30-day deletion window (R-17) cannot rely on freezing alone if money may still arrive. Money due to a frozen wallet waits in WAWU's merchant wallet and is paid on unfreeze, or the window does not freeze. No ruling changes; each task handles the case. **MONEY-18 (10 Oct 2026):** a release or a refund into a frozen wallet is refused by the provider (Fintava `400 "Kindly confirm both customer accounts are active"`, kind `wallet_inactive`); the hold stays `held`, the money stays in WAWU's account, `waiting: recipient_frozen`, and the sweep tries again hourly until the wallet is unfrozen (no review bound, nothing is refunded instead). A person with no wallet is `waiting: payee_wallet` or `payer_wallet`, tried every minute. WALLET-16 and SETTINGS-06 still handle their own halves. **SETTINGS-06 (16 Oct 2026, branch `wmt/SETTINGS-06`):** the deletion window freezes the Naira wallet through the provider seam (`freezeWallet`/`unfreezeWallet`, Fintava adapter; Nuvion has no freeze yet, so under Nuvion nothing is frozen), and a cancel unfreezes it. Money due to the frozen wallet waits in WAWU's account and is paid on unfreeze by the MONEY-18 hold sweep (and WALLET-16's earning sweep once it merges). The deletion is also refused up front while a hold is open or earnings wait, and the final purge re-checks the money and keeps the account if any appeared (`AccountDeletion.purgeBlockedBy`), so the freeze is not the only guard. | OPS-02 builder (re-run), 2 Oct 2026 | MONEY-18 (holds and refunds), WALLET-16 (earnings landing), SETTINGS-06 (deletion window) |

| G-16 | sign-up, sign-in (phone lookups) | `register` stores the phone exactly as typed and checks duplicates exactly, so the same number can sit on two web accounts (`0803...` and `+234...`), and any lookup by phone then finds one of them arbitrarily. A data gap, not a sign-up defect (R-36): sign-up refuses a number held by any spelling, but the existing double rows are not merged. Unblocked when AUTH-05 decides which row keeps the number and merges or releases the other. **AUTH-05 (default, agent; owner may override):** the row that keeps the number is (1) the one that proved the phone, else (2) the one that signed in most recently (newest refresh token), else (3) the oldest; every other row in the set releases it (its phone becomes `released:<its id>`, as anonymised rows get `deleted:<id>`; email, password and everything else stay, so it still signs in by email). wawu-id `scripts/data/g16-duplicate-phones.mjs`: `report` only reads; `apply --owner-approved` logs each old phone in `g16_phone_releases` in the same transaction; `revert --owner-approved` puts them back (refuses if any is held by another row by then). Tested on a local scratch database only (3 sets, 4 rows released, revert byte-identical); NEVER run on live data. Unblocked when the owner runs `report` on production, reads it, and approves or changes the rule. **AUTH-05 fix round 1 (verifier U1):** the rule never releases an account whose phone is its only way in (no email, no password, or an email never confirmed): such a row is preferred as the keeper, and when two of them share a number the other is listed by `report` under "owner to decide" and left alone by `apply`. `revert` refuses when the number is held, in any spelling, by a row other than the set's recorded keeper and the rows being restored. wawu-id never hands the `released:<id>` marker on as a phone: tokens and the account answer carry an empty phone, and no reset code is sent to it (`src/auth/released-phone.ts`). Scratch run: 5 sets, 5 released, 1 held, a clashing revert refused with nothing changed, the real revert byte-identical. Real data stays an owner stop. | AUTH-03 verifier (F-15), 2 Oct 2026 | AUTH-05 designed it; the owner runs it (rewrites real user rows: WORKFLOW section 10) |

Built (OPS-11, `wmt/OPS-11`, 2 Oct 2026): the Flutterwave webhook skips every named throttler, and `trust proxy` trusts exactly one hop, only from a loopback peer (nginx), so each caller has its own bucket and a direct connection's `X-Forwarded-For` is ignored; the app still listens on every interface, which ufw closes (`deploy/provision.sh`), and the droplet's live `ufw status` was not read. Since MONEY-17 round 7 the provider's own charge decides the figures, so a sandbox payment whose answer arrives completes with both ledger rows at what Fintava took (fee 0, total the price) and the difference (`debit differs from the quote: Fintava took 100000 kobo (fee 0), quoted 102325 kobo (fee 2325)`) on the payment's `WalletPayment.discrepancy`, nothing flagged; a charge above the quote completes both ledger rows at the real total too (lead ruling, 10 Oct 2026) and flags the payment (`debitReviewSince`, both figures on `discrepancy`) for NUV-08. Fintava's answer to the send carries the charge; its lookups and history do not, so when the answer is lost the payment completes at the quote unless Fintava's signed `wallet_to_wallet_transfer_v2` webhook reports the charge (round 8, R7-1: then at the reported figures, the same function). Live, with the dashboard rates, the figures agree. Seen in the sandbox on 3 Oct 2026 (`docs/fintava/sandbox/41-money17-pay-from-wallet.md`). | G-72 | A6, A7 (the selfie match and the account opening, after A26) | **Parts (a) and (b): served by KYC-03 round 2 with the sealed check handle (Lead ruling: option (b)); part (c) stays open.** Was: the two calls after A26 asked the app for the BVN and the NIN again, and the app no longer has them. `POST /money/identity/selfie` requires `bvn` (`SelfieMatchDto`) and `POST /money/wallet/open` requires `bvn` and `nin` (`OpenNairaWalletDto`); the server keeps only keyed hashes and the last 4 digits (CONVENTIONS section 8), so today the numbers must come from the phone. But A26 (KYC-05) sends them once, to the BVN check, and nothing on the phone keeps them after that request; KYC-03 does not re-ask for them (the brief: never re-ask, never store). So KYC-03 sends `{ image }` to the match and `{ firstName, lastName, dateOfBirth, address }` to the opening (`src/features/open-wallet/api/opening.ts`), and today's Hub refuses both with `400` (`forbidNonWhitelisted` validation, `bvn` missing): no account can open from the app. Needed, one of: (a) the Hub takes the BVN and the NIN from the person's passed check: it holds them encrypted (not hashed; a key outside the database, like `IDENTITY_HASH_KEY`), tied to that check (`bvnVerifiedAt`, `bvnHash`), deleted when the account opens, when another check passes, and after a PROVISIONAL 24 hours, never logged or answered; both DTOs drop the fields (the selfie compares against `checkedIdentity` as the opening already does). Or (b) the BVN check answers an opaque sealed handle (the numbers encrypted under a server key, with the owner and the check's time), which the app holds in memory beside the prefill (`keptCheck.ts`, forgotten on every session end) and sends back instead of the numbers. (a) is what the app is built against; (b) is a one-file change in `api/opening.ts`. Either keeps the numbers off the phone after A26; (a) changes KYC-01's storage rule ("never the full BVN"), so it is the owner's call. Also (c): a BVN record without a first name, last name or date of birth in a form the Hub reads (`BvnPrefillView` fields null) cannot open an account (the opening requires all three); A5 tells the person so before any selfie is charged ("We can't open an account from this BVN"), and nothing says what they do next (manual review, or typing it, are owner calls). **Served (KYC-03 round 2, backend `wmt/KYC-03` e769347, contract on the mobile branch):** the passed BVN check answers `checkHandle`, AES-256-GCM sealed `{ sub, bvn, nin, checkId, iat, exp }` under an HKDF key from `IDENTITY_HASH_KEY` (label `wawu/kyc-check-handle/v1`, no new setting), good for 30 minutes; the selfie match and the opening take it in place of the numbers (the numbers still work, additive); any handle that cannot be used is `409 bvn_not_checked` "Check your BVN again to continue." and the app starts the person again at A26. Nothing new is stored on either side; the app keeps the handle in memory with the prefill and drops it on every session end. **Still open: (c)**, a BVN record without a readable first name, last name or date of birth cannot open an account, and nothing says what the person does next (owner call: manual review, or let them type it). | KYC-03 builder, 3 Oct 2026 | MONEY-12 and KYC-02 (a, b: served by KYC-03 round 2); (c): owner to assign |

Reproduction (WALLET-27 verifier round 3, `scratchpad/vw27r3/xaddr.js`, 60 s each): a victim at 2 requests a second to `/health` gets 120 x 200 alone, and 6 x 429 while another address sends bursts of 25 every 1.5 s; the same on backend main 9a370c0 and on `wmt/WALLET-27`. Not fixed in WALLET-27 (it is the app's global throttler, not the statement route). | G-198 | H33, H36 (which reports are counted) | `POST /ads/:id/events` counts a report only if GET /ads could serve THAT card to THIS viewer right now, by the one shared rule (`servableCampaigns` in `src/ads/ads-serving.ts`): placement, status `scheduled` or `live`, `startsAt <= now < endsAt`, a creative, the card's event open (published, not called off, not over) and its host not blocked either way with the viewer. So it is "servable to this viewer now", not "the placement's current winner": a heavier booking made since does not stop the card on screen being counted. Anything else is one plain 404 that says nothing about why, including a tap that arrives after the event closed. There is no grace after the end: a report that reaches the server after the window closed, an admin paused the campaign or the event closed is not counted. A tap needs no earlier view, so a client that never sends views could make the tap rate above 100%; the rate is `null` when there are no views. **Known edge F1 (verifier):** a report already in flight when a campaign is paused or its event cancelled can still be counted after the pause committed (reproduced with a table lock; every report that STARTS after the pause is refused). The one-statement fix would be a `FOR SHARE` lock on the campaign row inside the insert's transaction, so that a pause (which locks the campaign row) waits for reports in flight; behaviour is NOT changed, the owner decides. **U4 (verifier):** a lighter booking that is permanently outranked can be inflated by any account, because the server keeps no record of what it served (only a signed or recorded serve could fix that). Unblocked by: the owner saying whether a late report (the card was on screen when the window closed or the event closed) should count and for how long, whether only the placement's current winner should count (stricter), whether F1 should be closed with the lock, and whether outranked bookings must be protected from inflation (U4). | ADS-05 builder, 6 Oct 2026 | owner |

(d) `DELETE /push-tokens` carries the token in a JSON body (kept out of URLs and access logs); some HTTP clients and proxies drop a DELETE body. | G-641 | S4 (the name AND address under the meter number) | Fintava's `POST /billing/preview-meter` success body has never been seen: no sandbox meter number is known (question 11, `docs/fintava/naira-api.md`), and every made-up meter answers `400`. Round 2 (verifier D2): the Hub answers a meter as found ONLY for a `data` object that names an owner as text under one of a list of likely keys (`customerName`, `name`, `accountName`, `consumerName`, `fullName`, `customerFullName` for the name; `address`, `customerAddress`, `consumerAddress`, `serviceAddress` for the address; capitals and separators ignored, `readMeterAnswer` in `src/fintava/fintava-meter-answer.ts`) and only when that name is not the company's own. Fintava's failure styles inside a 2xx map to a refusal (`"service not currently available"` to `503 bills_unavailable`, a body whose `status` is 400 or whose message says no such meter to `422 meter_not_found`, an empty `data` to `422 meter_not_found`), and any other body (not JSON, `data` a string, an array or an object with no name, an owner nested somewhere unknown, a name that is the company's own) to the new `502 meter_unconfirmed`, which S4 words as "We couldn't confirm this meter." with Try again. The Hub never makes a name up and never answers a found meter without one. Round 3 (R2-1, lead's ruling), made a rule in round 4 (R3-1 and R3-2, lead's ruling): until the real body is seen, a found meter also needs a body with no failure signal anywhere in it or in anything nested in it (an explicit no under `success`, `valid`, `verified`, `found`, `exists`, `ok` or `status`; a `status`, `message` or `error` that says failed, invalid, unable, not found, unavailable and the like, in any case, as text or in a list; an `error` or `errors` that holds anything; a result code of 400 or more, or a small one other than 0) and an owner name that is a person's: once invisible characters are out and case, width and spaces are folded, it is not made only of placeholder words (null, none, nil, unknown, undefined, n/a in every spelling, the words of "no customer found" or "meter not registered", numbers; `PLACEHOLDER_WORDS` in `src/fintava/fintava-meter-answer.ts`) or of the words a company's name is made of (plc, limited, distribution, company, disco, electric, electricity, prepaid, bill, payment; `COMPANY_FORM_WORDS`) and the chosen company's own words (its name, code and short names such as IKEDC), and it is not a sentence saying there is no such meter or that the service is off; one word off those lists makes it an owner ("Ada Unknown Eze", "Nil Okoro", "Ikeja Ada"). Anything else is the same `502 meter_unconfirmed`. The name and the address are read only to a byte bound (`BILLS_PROVIDER_TEXT_MAX_BYTES`, 1,024, PROVISIONAL) before the 200 character cut, after the spaces and invisible characters at their start are taken off; a name with nothing a person can see left is `502 meter_unconfirmed` and an address like that is none (R3-6). The success path is tested against stand-in bodies that are labelled guesses. Unblocked by: Fintava naming a sandbox meter, or a first real preview; then the key lists, the placeholder words and the failure signals are cut to the real ones and this row closes. | BILLS-01 builder, 10 Oct 2026 (round 3, 11 Oct; round 4, 11 Oct) | none yet (OPS-02 re-run with a real meter) |
